# SP103_CW_36 — Changes and decisions

The manuscript images decide the text. C and H are the supplied AI witnesses; S identifies the supplied sweep rows. Macro spelling, wrappers that do not change a reading, and H’s editorial notes are excluded from substantive difference counts. A retained C doubt or an S check may have no C/H-change flag; its row remains an explicit reading decision. Counts are places, not words. Confidence concerns the recorded final reading, including any explicit uncertainty. An em dash in a witness or final-reading cell means that no reading or text is recorded there.

Its upper tip and lower turn admit a looped ell as well as a cancelled parenthesis; no extra formula argument is legible. The whole E/H formula has been checked.
- Scan 16, after signifie aussi que: `\deleted{{\unsure{H|W}}^{\handwritten{2n-1}}(}`. A capital with upright and cross-stroke survives under the first x, favouring H; the cancellation can also create a W-like outline. The penned exponent 2n-1 is clear. A curved opening mark at the right is crossed.
- Scan 17, first vanishing-cohomology formula: `E^{2n-1}(Y_{\bar s})`. The enlarged capital has a single continuous upright and ordinary serifed top, middle and lower arms. Weight alone does not identify two impressions. The whole formula reads exponent 2n-1 and a separate short bar over s.

### Remaining unresolved places

- top centre: `short irregular upright mark`; alternatives ["ink mark", "numeral 1", "scan/physical blemish"]. No distinctive numeral head or foot; dark, isolated, kinked stroke retained as a mark.
- after fixé: `live x`; alternatives ["x used as cancellation mark"]. The terminal x has the complete typewriter x contours and no distinct overlaid strike or recoverable comma. Accept live x; a possible cancellation function is noted separately.
- reference 5): `unreadable overtyped predecessor`; alternatives ["4", "3", "another digit"]. Excess strokes remain, but neither complete predecessor outline survives.
- struck théorè: `téhroè`; alternatives ["théorè"]. The accented second position and the following crossed bodies favour the malformed start téhroè, with théorè retained as alternative. Keep the live théorème and its doubtful old p; no extra terminal accent is present.
- m position of live théorème: `p overwritten by m`; alternatives ["unidentified overtyped letter"]. A descender and partial bowl remain below/left of m.
- after first E^{2n-1}: `cancelled opening parenthesis`; alternatives ["ell"]. Looped/curved stem and crossing strokes; whole formula checked.
- after symétries at right edge: `donc`; alternatives ["dont"]. Terminal glyph damaged/clipped; text does not decide the ink.
- cela ... signifie: `est implique que H`; alternatives ["xxx implique que H"]. Three crossed positions before implique retain traces compatible with est, especially the final upright t. Prefer doubtful est over bare xxx. The later cancelled formula still shows an opening parenthesis, not an established coefficient subscript; retain its H/W doubt.
- struck formula before H^{p,q}: `H^{2n-1}(`; alternatives ["W^{2n-1}("]. Initial capital is overstruck; exponent clear and parenthesis crossed.
- de before hypersurfaces: `live de`; alternatives ["struck de"]. Overlap comes from the close insertion line; no independent complete cancellation stroke is visible through de.
- extra crossed letter in hypersurfaces: `e`; alternatives ["r"]. Keep de live: the apparent apostrophe is the descending insertion stroke. Within hypersurfaces there is a crossed extra letter between hyp and ersurfaces, with a curved e-like body under the x; record e/r doubt.
- struck verification phrase: `vérffier`; alternatives ["vérifier"]. Two adjacent f-shaped upper hooks appear after vér in the crossed word. Prefer the written malformed vérffier, retaining vérifier as the obscured-letter alternative.
- start of crossed sufficiency phrase: `, il suffit`; alternatives ["il suffit"]. A comma-like descending remnant precedes il. The crossed opening and the accent over vérifier are not fully separable, so accept the blind reader’s explicit uncertainties with the visible insertion unchanged.
- crossed vérifier in sufficiency phrase: `vérifier`; alternatives ["verifier"]. A comma-like descending remnant precedes il. The crossed opening and the accent over vérifier are not fully separable, so accept the blind reader’s explicit uncertainties with the visible insertion unchanged.

### Remaining unresolved places

- upper margin: `non`; alternatives []. Rapid open humps; first word remains uncertain after crop 01.
- left theorem margin: `pas prouvé; sous cette`; alternatives []. Opening and beginning of second line touch the clipped left edge; forte is clear.
- prov + cancelled character + enant: `t`; alternatives ["i"]. A short upper transverse bar projects from the crossed predecessor after prov, favouring t over the first i. The middle is covered by the x strike, so retain i as an alternative rather than claiming a certain t.
- Gal formula: `G'`; alternatives ["G"]. Extra upright trace after G may be a prime or residual type impression. The blind proposed b has no independently clear bowl; first uncertainty retained.
- dimension, local type correction: `old e under m; old m under e`; alternatives []. The inserted V has the small round subscript o. The first m position in dimension also contains a rounded old e-like trace under its shoulders, before the already-recorded old m beneath the following e. Add that predecessor with doubt and retain both correction layers.
- formé + cancelled character: `s`; alternatives ["e"]. The separate crossed character after formé has upper and lower curved remnants favouring s. A middle e-like return remains possible under the x. Use s&#124;e rather than resolving the obscured character outright.
- cancelled finite-field superscript: `*`; alternatives ["\\times"]. Blackened rays do not identify the original sign. Cancellation itself is certain.
- handwritten trace insertion stabilizer: `u`; alternatives ["G_o", "G"]. The expanded crop (340,1420,1150,1538) restores the insertion end. The first joined loop and crossed upright give et, followed by a separate un. The noun has the e-s-p-a-c-e sequence and one medial descender, not the two expected for groupe. Resolve et un and sous-espace. The final stabilizer remains obscured by nearby strokes: u is favoured, with G_o/G alternatives. Both V subscripts are round o.
- cancelled cardinality after p: `-1`; alternatives ["+1"]. Blackened local cancellation leaves only short horizontal and upright strokes.
- cancelled ne before sont: `ne`; alternatives ["xx"]. Only fragments of the underlying two letters remain.
- cancelled old parenthesis after semi-simples: `)`; alternatives []. Curved upright partly masked by pen scribble.
- left corollary margin: `pas prouvé; est; à`; alternatives []. The left edges of these words are physically clipped; équivalent, sa and forte can be read.
- inserted and cancelled fermé: `fermé`; alternatives []. Initial f and a run of joined letters survive; cancellation prevents letter-by-letter certainty.
- final inserted parenthesis: `avec`; alternatives ["où"]. Handwriting crosses cancelled type; four linked letters are suggested but not securely isolated.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Top-left cancelled fragment: `card(x`; alternatives ["card("]. The final x may belong to the machine cancellation; no contextual completion is made.
- First display, old field argument: `(t))`; alternatives ["t))"]. The opening parenthesis overlaps the live handwritten correction.
- X field subscript, old argument: `(t)`; alternatives []. Old t and right curve are secure; opening curve is crossed by pen strokes.
- Cancelled word before égal: `confru`; alternatives ["congru"]. Fourth position has a high f-like hook; x-striking still obscures letters, so no complete resolution is claimed.
- Machine-cancelled phrase before Quitte: `et même`; alternatives ["et ainsi"]. e and tall t are plausible; the second word is crossed by machine x marks and a pen line. The second alternative was supplied by main reader after independent crop review.
- Target capital of last homomorphism: `U overwritten by Y`; alternatives []. Y fork/stem are clear; low U bowl and uprights survive, but the overlapping impression reduces certainty about the predecessor.
- After l in last rationnels: `cancelled n`; alternatives ["cancelled e"]. The old initial d has a tall ascender and complete lower bowl beneath the live r, so d is resolved. The separately crossed letter after l has vertical remnants favouring n, but an e remains possible beneath the x. The expanded crop (990,1985,1460,2100) shows the complete live prime and old (t).
- Thick hook inside handwritten monomorphism insertion: `leader/correction stroke; no recoverable word`; alternatives ["H suggests one cancelled illegible word"]. The hook rises from after pour toward the formula and joins the insertion boundary. No distinct letter remains. Following main crop review, no invented deleted word is inserted; H's editorial ill is not treated as source text.
- Crossed typed word before fundamental-group pi: `de`; alternatives ["du"]. The second letter of the crossed typed d-word is partly covered by the x strike; e is favoured but u cannot be excluded. The phrase before Quitte has recoverable traces supporting et même / et ainsi, so it is not replaced wholesale with an illegible span.
- Old article under handwritten le before point: `un`; alternatives ["la"]. The new looped l and e are clear. Beneath them, the first low curved type remnant favours u and the second has an upright; the overlaid loops also permit la. The old un cannot remain certain, and the blind ta is not independently established. Add un&#124;la uncertainty.

### Remaining unresolved places

- initial of second Elément: `deleted é followed by E`; alternatives ["É", "E"]. Upper and lower impressions overlap; accent state not separable.
- initial of Frob_k: `deleted f followed by F`; alternatives ["f"]. Descender and high bars overlap.
- relation c+(-1)^{n-1}Tr f ... a mod p: `cancelled minus below live equals`; alternatives ["cancelled x"]. Two clean live horizontal bars stand above a separately x-cancelled short horizontal. Resolve live equals; retain minus/x uncertainty for the cancelled lower mark.
- a in Čebotâref: `old ä beneath â`; alternatives ["old é beneath â"]. The live a has a joined circumflex; above it are two earlier accent traces and an offset old body. Preserve a cancelled accented letter, uncertain between ä and é, before live â.
- cancelled qui rencontre ...: `un U with handwritten subscript k-prime`; alternatives ["U-prime", "subscript k"]. The cancelled type reads un before a separate U-like expression with a lowered k-prime/k. The original merged these. The expanded crop also shows a hollow circular callout with an up-right stem after D in U-prime(k-prime), not a comma.
- marginal extension abbreviation: `quele.`; alternatives ["qqle.", "quelc."]. One clear q descender, low middle humps, tall l, and final e-like loop. A second q descender is not clear.
- before cycles: `deleted s`; alternatives []. One s-like body under an x overstrike.
- old first niveau inequality: `deleted ≤`; alternatives ["deleted <"]. The live pen inequality has a lower bar. The old crossed angular mark might be less-than with or without its lower bar; add the less-than alternative.
- et before cancelled est ramené: `on overwritten by il`; alternatives ["il overwritten by on"]. Both the o/n bodies and the i/l stems are present. Their relative layer order is not secure from overlap, so retain both possibilities. The crossed est ramené à is readable through the machine x row.
- final cancelled handwritten expression: `H_t^i`; alternatives ["E_t^i"]. A raised dotted i and a lowered t with crossbar are recoverable beside the crossed central letter. Add these indices; H versus E remains uncertain beneath the crossing strokes.
- long note comme l’ouvert: `l’ouvert`; alternatives []. Tall initial and apostrophe visible; middle joins compressed.
- long note convenable: `convenable`; alternatives []. con- and b/l/e ending visible, intervening joins compressed.
- comma after cancelled qui: `comma`; alternatives ["nonpunctuating overstrike remnant"]. Dark short descending stroke lies below the baseline immediately after qui; it may be a typed comma beneath the long crossing line.
- long note passage: `passage`; alternatives ["pass."]. The first article and compressed convenable remain uncertain. The bar ends above k, before the prime. Passage has crowded final joins, so pass. is retained as an alternative. The apparent underline of variante is the enclosing frame crossing beneath the word; a separate pale lower stroke is drawn separately. The right frame has a small pen gap. No final stop is visible after Bertini.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Opening coefficient; recurring glyph on page: `\ell`; alternatives ["l"]. Crossed long stem and foot allow both letter conventions; first occurrence marked, later occurrences use ell.
- Overtype inside théorème: `\deleted{p}m`; alternatives ["\\deleted{n}m", "m"]. The descender favours p, but superposition makes the predecessor uncertain.
- Clipped marginal continuation below faible: `e`; alternatives []. Only an e-like portion survives; no unshown t is supplied.
- Inserted factor before last first-line Trace: `(-1)^{n-1}`; alternatives []. The small raised exponent is faint; C’s tentative n−1 remains.
- Cancelled start of second display line: `\deleted{+(-1)}`; alternatives []. Prefix is densely crossed and exact symbol extent remains uncertain.
- New second-line display sign: `(-1)^{n-1}`; alternatives []. Visible (-1), compact and faint superscript interpreted tentatively as n−1.
- Second displayed trace: `\mathbb{E}`; alternatives ["\\deleted{H}E", "E"]. The cancelled prefix has more than the two type positions required for et and curved/vertical remnants supporting +(-1); its exact scope remains doubtful. The live E has extra upper and right contours which could belong to an old H as well as a doubled E. Add that alternative without resolving the layer.
- After remplacer k: `\deleted{t}`; alternatives ["\\deleted{s}"]. The crossed remnant is lowered to the right of k. A faint upper bar and foot favour old t, while s is possible beneath the x cancellation. Encode a cancelled subscript rather than treating the cancelling x as the sole old glyph.
- Old verb below sont: `\deleted{étaient}`; alternatives []. Initial two letters remain largely hidden by x overstrikes; not every letter is independently plain.
- After on en conclut: `\deleted{ait que pour}`; alternatives ["\\deleted{alors que pour}"]. The first crossed group after the live verb shows an a-like bowl followed by upright remnants favouring ait; the older alors estimate remains possible under the repeated x strikes. Que and pour are separately readable. Mark only the first group doubtful and preserve the rest.
- After second card Y_s formula: `\deleted{x}`; alternatives ["live x", "cancellation keystroke without recoverable predecessor"]. Bare x-shaped impression; Main retained the tentative cancelled state; the live-x alternative remains documented.
- Overtype in choisira/choisita: `choisi\deleted{r}ta`; alternatives ["choisi\\deleted{t}ra"]. Both t and r contours occur, but their typing order is not established.
- Cancelled handwritten insertion after ∈U′(k′): `\deleted{passant}`; alternatives []. The crossed pen word starts with a descending p, an a-like loop and low curves, and ends with a tall t. This supports passant rather than convenable, which would require internal b/l ascenders not present. The middle is crossed, so retain a doubt on passant.
- Base field after display: `k(\deleted{t}s)`; alternatives ["underlying letter uncertain"]. Both the live s curves and a t-like upper upright are present.
- Preposition before choix: `d\deleted{u}e choix`; alternatives ["underlying letter uncertain"]. Additional stem/curve impression beneath current e.
- Point after de choix de: `\deleted{t}s`; alternatives ["underlying letter uncertain"]. Centered upright/top serif under s.
- Second card formula point subscript: `Y_{\deleted{t}s}`; alternatives ["underlying letter uncertain"]. Same upper upright plus s curves seen in crop20.
- Cancelled impression after au: `\deleted{\unsure{é&#124;x}}`; alternatives ["\\deleted{é}", "\\deleted{x}"]. The blind audit of 021-b03 retained an acute and curved e-like remnant above the cancelling x; the old letter remains compatible with é or x. Both alternatives remain cancelled. The completed second reading does not resolve the letter.
- Opening Y, older superscript: `it`; alternatives []. Above the new bar over the subscript t are two faint older raised strokes, including a dot and an upright/crossbar. Add the cancelled superscript, tentatively it. The live bar-t and underlined Q agree; ell/l remains uncertain.
- Point after imposer à: `old t beneath s`; alternatives []. Above the darker live s is an older centered upright with a crossbar, matching the other t-to-s corrections on this page. Add old t with doubt; D intersection U(k-prime) is unchanged.

### Certain source slips and mathematical warnings

- First prose congruence paragraph: `soit égales`; confidence high. Certain literal source slip: dot above i, not n; expected sont is not transcribed. Blind audit additionally recovers old u under the live i.
- End of line before de s, soit c: `de choix`; confidence high. Certain literal source slip: e is visible; expected du is not transcribed.

The original relation is merged with the long strike; add equals to the unresolved greater-or-equal/minus alternatives without restoring a mathematical condition.

Record the local overtype alternatives explicitly instead of flattening the whole word to a normal spelling or an extra i.
- Predecessor under final s of les frobenius: `f`; alternatives []. A tall hook and crossbar suggest f under the pen s.
- Dense last letter of points in sur des points femmés: `old e under live s`; alternatives []. An extra rounded body with a horizontal middle bar survives under the final s in points. Add an old e with doubt. The literal femmés and the upper diophantiennes insertion remain unchanged.
- Older inequality beneath live isomorphism bound: `\deleted{\geq}`; alternatives []. The original and expanded crop show the live left-pointing inequality above an older right-pointing angular outline. Add the cancelled older greater-or-equal with doubt. The upper stroke is now fully visible; the live lower bar remains clear.

### Remaining unresolved places

- first cancelled phrase after somme alternée: `\deleted{\unsure{des valeurs}}`; alternatives []. The x overstrike hides several letter joins; des valeurs is the best visible reading but is not resolved.
- next cancelled phrase at start of following line: `\deleted{\unsure{valeurs des du nombre}}`; alternatives []. The long phrase is supported, but the start of valeurs and some middle joins are covered. Added uncertainty only.
- pen insertion above crossed alors: `\deleted{alors}\deleted{\inserted{\handwritten{\unsure{toujours&#124;encore}}}}`; alternatives ["encore"]. The crossed interlinear group has more humps than encore, an initial upright with a crossbar and a possible central j descender. Toujours is preferred with encore retained as an alternative. Its interlinear placement and separate cancellation show an insertion subsequently struck.
- left display label: `\deleted{\unsure{***&#124;xxx}}`; alternatives ["***", "xxx"]. Three cancelled x-like shapes; short middle strokes do not decide asterisks versus x characters.
- handwritten stable in clause et V-prime_o subset U_o / V_o stable par f: `\unsure{stable}`; alternatives []. Initial curve and tall middle strokes support stable, but the compressed joins are faint.
- handwritten insertion before Soit r: `On \unsure{peut supposer} $X$ réduit.`; alternatives []. On and X réduit. are clear. The flattened peut and joined descending pp of supposer support the phrase, without fully exposing every join. This added doubt is not a new change row.
- cancelled component designation in lower draft: `X_{\unsure{1&#124;i}}`; alternatives ["1", "i"]. Exposed stem has a top serif and foot; the location of a possible i dot is occupied by x cancellation.
- cancelled clause at source right edge after qui: `\unsure{x_i&#124;x_1}`; alternatives ["x_i", "x_1"]. The lowered serif/stem and crossed baseline mark are source-clipped; no further characters are supplied.
- Second handwritten capital in stable-subspace insertion: `U_o`; alternatives ["V_o"]. The first V-prime has an acute lower angle; the next capital has near-vertical sides and a rounded bowl, favouring U over V. Record U_o with V_o as an alternative. Stable remains compressed and uncertain. The mathematical expectation V_o does not override the pen shape.
- Pen ending after opening p-linéaire: `s`; alternatives [")", ">"]. The pen ending is a long descending hook with a short left return, compatible with the abbreviated s reading but also with a closing parenthesis or angle. Retain s as the earlier reading with the visible hook alternatives; do not use plural grammar to remove the doubt.

### Certain source slips and mathematical warnings

- second display: `N\handwritten{\geq}rd_1^n`; confidence high. Not a reading doubt: the right-pointing angle and separate equality bar are clear. The S warning concerns mathematical intention (≤), not ambiguous ink; ≥ is retained.
- live clause following cancellation: `les composantes irréductibles X_i de X géométriquement irréductibles`; confidence high. Not a reading doubt: the source has no visible soient. The grammatical gap is preserved without supplying a word.

### Remaining unresolved places

- after tous: `es`; alternatives []. Two letter positions are crossed; e/s contours remain partial.
- Quitte initial capital: `\deleted{S}Q`; alternatives []. An S-like curve survives at lower left, but layers overlap.
- ending added to tel: `le`; alternatives ["l", "e"]. Tall loop and small terminal curve; the number of written letters remains open.
- first auxiliary form: `\psi`; alternatives ["\\mu"]. Slanted stem and low returning arms; repeated copies use first candidate without repeated doubt marks.
- bound, before subscript 2: `d`; alternatives ["\\phi"]. Loop and tall slant fit either glyph; mathematical degree context alone favours d.
- cancelled fragment after 7.6: `(donc`; alternatives []. Opening parenthesis-like curve and word skeleton under x row.
- cancelled clause after et de r: `lorsqu'on désire dans`; alternatives []. Dense machine cancellation leaves incomplete letters.

### Certain source slips and mathematical warnings

- empty-set notation: `\phi`; confidence high. Literal slashed oval matches phi form glyph; mathematical role is the empty set.
- product equality source slip: `(H_{\phi\psi}\cap X)(k)=Z(k)`; confidence high. Z(k) is plainly written. Since the earlier construction gives (H_n∩Z)(k), the right member appears mathematically overlarge; it is preserved.
- unfinished parenthesis: `(en prenant, comme il est loisible, $d_2=n+1$.`; confidence high. A closing parenthesis is absent in the source.

### Remaining unresolved places

- Opening paragraph, first affaablie/affaiblie: `\unsure{affaablie&#124;affaiblie}`; alternatives ["affaablie", "affaiblie"]. Overlapping a-shaped bowls and dotted/upright remnants prevent separating the impressions. Later clean affaiblie gives linguistic context only.
- b), first product second factor: `\unsure{d''_2&#124;d'_2}`; alternatives ["d''_2", "d'_2"]. Two dark adjacent vertical remnants and merged upper ink leave the number and state of the prime strokes unresolved.
- b), parenthetical first subscript: `d'_{\unsure{\deleted{1}2&#124;\deleted{2}1}}`; alternatives ["d'_{\\deleted{1}2}", "d'_{\\deleted{2}1}"]. A 1-like upright/foot and a curved/diagonal 2 overlap; chronology cannot be established visually.
- b), above the gap after rationnels: `Raised bent pen stroke after rationnels`; alternatives ["function as proofreading mark unresolved"]. The raised dark mark has a comparatively long lower bend followed by an upright, rather than a recoverable punctuation glyph. Replace the tentative apostrophe with the visible bent pen stroke. Its proofreading function remains unknown.
- Final group at the lower edge: `\unsure{G_{s\,o}&#124;G_s,}`; alternatives ["G_{s\\,o}", "G_s,"]. At the physical lower edge, only a curved upper fragment survives after G_s. A round subscript o remains the first candidate, but a following comma cannot be excluded from this fragment alone. Mark both readings and supply no missing continuation.
- cancelled raised sign after V near the lower edge: `\deleted{$\unsure{V'&#124;V^+}$}`; alternatives ["V'", "V^+"]. The G compound index contains t then round o without a comma. The cancelled V is identifiable under X. Its raised upright has a short horizontal crossing, compatible with a crossed prime or a plus; retain V-prime first but mark V-plus as an alternative.

### Remaining unresolved places

- initial of via: `\deleted{\unsure{n&#124;v}}\handwritten{v}`; alternatives ["deleted n under live pen v", "deleted v under live pen v"]. The thick live v covers the older typed shoulders and middle strokes. The old o under i in the following position is clear and separately preserved.
- avec: `\deleted{\unsure{e}}a\deleted{t}ve\deleted{d}c`; alternatives ["e under a", "an unresolved older contour under a"]. The t/v and d/c overtypes are distinct; the first e/a overlap leaves the exact older e less secure. The word is not reduced to uncorrected avec.
- prefix before cristallin in handwritten heading: `\unsure{torsi-&#124;tori-}`; alternatives ["torsi-", "tori-"]. The first glyph has a long upright crossed by a separate bar, supporting t rather than q. A definite detached hyphen follows the compact middle joins. No p descender or full final s of topos appears. The compressed middle can still be read rsi or ri, so retain torsi-/tori-.
- final handwritten parenthesis: `\unsure{notes&#124;note}`; alternatives ["notes", "note"]. The end is one flattened connected stroke with a small terminal hook, not a securely separate s.

### Certain source slips and mathematical warnings

- last heading phrase: `le cohomologie`; confidence high. Not a reading doubt: the final e is open and clear. The grammatical disagreement is preserved.
- first heading phrase spanning the line break: `le ... cristallin`; confidence high. Not a reading doubt: the first article is le and the following noun ends cristallin before a comma, without a terminal e.

## Independent blind second readings

First TSVs were frozen before assignment. Each second reader had only the target images and neutral coordinate boxes and had not performed that page’s first reading. Literal TeX tokens were compared by script, ignoring whitespace only. Every differing box was viewed again by the main reader. B is the number of boxes, D the number with any token difference, and K the number whose adjudication changed the transcription. Context extent, wrapper syntax and drawing code can produce a token difference without changing a manuscript reading. The full comparison JSON retains every token opcode.
